# Niconico

**Niconico** (ニコニコ動画, *Niko Niko Dōga*, niconico) is a Japanese video-sharing service operated by [[Dwango]]. It launched in 2006. Other services, including Niconico Live and Niconico Seiga, were subsequently developed; on May 1, 2012, these and Niconico Video became part of the video service “niconico” (renamed “ニコニコ” on October 27, 2020).[^tn-naming]

## Overview

Niconico Video is one of Japan's leading video-sharing sites and has generated numerous expressions and forms of culture. Its distinctive feature is a proprietary [[#Comment system|comment system]] that lets users post comments at any point on a video's playback timeline. Posted comments appear as captions scrolling from right to left [^10] [^11]. Users can also change their size, color, and other attributes [^10]. Many other features facilitate interaction among viewers and uploaders. The site has attracted worldwide attention as a center for the dissemination of Japanese culture; in China, the imitator services AcFun and bilibili were launched as video sites allowing users to post *danmaku* (“barrage”) comments and similar content [^12] [^13].[^tn-danmaku]

Niconico Video's guiding principles are set out in the “Niconico Declaration (9),” issued by the Niconico Promotion Committee on June 1, 2007, and revised on June 1, 2010 [^14] [^1].

The service received a Good Design Award for fiscal 2007 [^16] and the 2007 Japan Otaku Award. In 2008, Ars Electronica awarded it an Honorary Mention in the Digital Communities category of the Prix Ars Electronica [^17] [^18].

The name “Niconico Video” reportedly arose against the background of copyright litigation involving video services before its launch. The idea was to choose “a deflating name, difficult to say and as unlikely as possible to attract anger, rather than a cool word.” Dwango's then chairman, Nobuo Kawakami, remarked: “You know those silly names that just put a respectable face on something that's obviously shady? Niconico Loans, Niconico Finance, things like that. So, something like Niconico Video.” [[Hiroyuki Nishimura|Hiroyuki Nishimura]] agreed: “That's funny! Definitely that!” and the name was chosen [^19].[^tn-naming]

## History

### 2006

- Dwango began brainstorming an Internet live-streaming service as the next generation of its mobile audio and image streaming service “Pakeraji” [^20] [^21].
- August — Dwango's Makoto Nakano thought of using YouTube videos and made the first prototype, “Pakeraji 2.0 (Net Live),” which displayed comments in a separate window to the right of a video sourced from YouTube [^22].
- UEI's Hidekazu Furukawa made a second prototype, “Pakeraji 2.0 (Net Live),” in which comments were overlaid on the video and scrolled from right to left, as a demonstration for [[Hiroyuki Nishimura|Hiroyuki Nishimura]], then a Niwango director/administrator and administrator of [[2channel Hub|2channel]] [^23]. Nishimura viewed “Pakeraji 2.0” in October [^21].
- October — On the instructions of then Dwango chairman Nobuo Kawakami, Dwango's Akihiko Koizuka made the third prototype, “Niwango Video (Niwavide),” which became the basis of Niconico Video's system [^24].
- December 1 — A kickoff meeting for the Niwavide project adopted “Niconico Video,” named by Hiroyuki Nishimura, as Niwango Video's provisional service name [^25]. The launch development team was reorganized to comprise engineers Akihiko Koizuka and Shinnosuke Suzuki and designer Naoto Nakagawa.
- December 5 — The nicovideo.jp domain was registered.
- December 10 — Videos sourced from AmebaVision were registered.
- December 12 — The service launched as **Niconico Video (Provisional)**. It allowed users to watch videos uploaded to YouTube and AmebaVision while typing comments over them [^26].
- December 15 — A ranking by total number of comments was added [^27].
- December 22 — A ranking by total views was added.

### 2007

- January 15 — **Niconico Video (Provisional)** [^28] ended and **Niconico Video (β)** launched [^29].
- February 7 — The service announced that monthly page views for January had exceeded 100 million.
- February 17 — It received ¥5,000 by redeeming points from Ameba's contributor rankings [^30].
- February 19 — “Photozou” was added to the supported video sites [^31].
- February 23 — The service was suspended because DoS attacks that had continued for roughly three days were also affecting [[Dwango|Dwango's]] mobile sites. Around the same time, YouTube blocked its access.
- February 24 — The service announced the end of its beta test and the start of development of a new version.
- March 3 — Applications for new “Niconico Video (γ)” IDs opened. A system failure occurred immediately afterward, but service resumed the same day.
- March 6 — **Niconico Video (γ)** launched, together with the video-upload site “[[SMILEVIDEO]].”
- March 6 — To celebrate the launch of Niconico Video (γ), dwango.jp distributed a *chaku-uta* ringtone version of “Let's Go! Onmyōji,” whose promotional video had become extremely popular on the site.[^tn-mobile]
- March 7 — At the request of CyberAgent, operator of AmebaVision, AmebaVision videos were temporarily removed from the homepage and rankings.
- March 13 — IDs usable only during specified hours were added. Subsequently, fixed numbers of both time-limited and unrestricted IDs were added regularly.
- March 29 — MicroAd advertisements appeared on viewing pages for videos submitted to Niconico Video (γ) from AmebaVision.
- May 7 — The free email portal Niwango began recruiting 5,000 testers for a closed beta of Niconico Video's mobile version [^32].
- May 19 — The Niconico Video bulletin board (later niconico Bulletin Board) opened.
- June 1 — The Niconico Promotion Committee issued the “Niconico Declaration (Provisional)” to all Internet users who wanted to smile.
- June 7 — **Niconico Bookmark**, allowing users to bookmark and review websites, launched [^33].
- June 18 — Niconico Video (γ) ended. After six hours of maintenance, from 09:00 to 15:00, **Niconico Video (RC)** and paid Premium membership launched. A My Memory feature was added to preserve comments users wanted to retain.
- July 9 — An original video featuring Democratic Party leader Ichirō Ozawa was published in a special section [^34].
- July 10 — Niconico Video Mobile au (Experimental) launched.
- July 12 — **Niconico Market (Provisional)** launched.
- July 15 — Video views exceeded one billion.
- July 18 — Dwango Music Publishing released the CD album *Shin Gōketsuji Ichizoku: Bonnō Kaihō*, with a bonus DVD, including “Let's Go! Onmyōji,” whose promotional video had become extremely popular on Niconico Video [^35].
- July 26 — Registered IDs exceeded two million, and Niconico Premium members exceeded 54,000 [^36].
- August 9 — Niconico Video Mobile opened to the public.
- August 22 — The login procedure changed for security reasons.
- August 25 — The web anime *Saishū Shiken Kujira* was distributed through the PC site “Chō! Animelo” [^37].
- September 7 — The web drama *Elnoot* was distributed [^38].
- Around September 21 — Registered IDs exceeded three million.
- September 27 — As Niconico Video's business expanded, Dwango announced an internal reorganization: the relevant departments of its Business Development Division and dwango.jp Business Headquarters would merge into a new Niconico Business Division effective October 1 [^39].
- September 28 — A partnership with Viacom International Japan was announced, under which Viacom would supply MTV Networks content to Niconico Video [^40].
- October 1 — AmebaVision ended support for Niconico Video.
- October 10 — The “Niconico Video (RC2) Presentation” was held at Akihabara UDX [^41].
- October 10 — **Niconico Video (RC2)** launched. Video-distribution partnerships with seven Japanese companies were also announced [^42]. Niconico Interruptions formally launched.
- October 18 — The Taiwanese version of Niconico Video launched.
- October 30 — The social bookmarking service “livedoor Clip” added support for Niconico Video.
- November 12 — Distribution began of promotional videos and original footage featuring artists belonging to Avex Entertainment.
- November 12 — A special remix project officially approved by Lantis anime-song group JAM Project began.
- November 22 — The AIC × DREAMUSIC web anime *Candy☆Boy* was distributed [^43].
- November 26 — The “Yoshiyoshi Video” section launched to jointly distribute comedy programming produced by Yoshimoto Kōgyō [^44] [^45] [^2].
- November 26 — Results of the first International Niconico Film Festival were announced.
- November 26 — dwango.jp and the Animelo mobile sites began distributing *chaku-uta* and *chaku-uta Full* versions of songs made with the popular voice-synthesis software Hatsune Miku, which had attracted attention on Niconico Video [^48].
- November 26 — Nicoscript launched.
- December 3 — Niconico Video Mobile launched “Nicofit” for au handsets, displaying clicks and calories burned [^49].
- December 12 — Niconico Video marked its first anniversary. A midnight time-signal advertisement announced it, prompting so-called “festivals” on prominent videos; some videos became unable to connect to the comment servers.[^tn-matsuri]
- December 12 — The former Niconico News (now **Niconico Info**) and **Niconicolumn** launched.
- December 25 — The first Niconico Live program, “Live Discussion!! Hiroyuki and Koizuka: The People Who Made Niconico,” aired [^50].
- December 31 — Anticipating a sharp increase in New Year traffic, the service briefly closed at 23:55 and resumed normal operation at 00:02 on January 1. Existing users were not disconnected during the “closure”; a dramatized “Niconico's Turbulent History” and countdown footage aired in the time-signal slot.

### 2008

- February 8 — An electronic noticeboard was installed to notify all viewers promptly of purchases of mobile content through Niconico Market.
- February 18 — Niconico Video Mobile became an official i-mode service.
- February 14 — Niconico News announced an upgrade from RC2 to SP1.
- February 22 — The live program “Niconico Market In-store Event,” featuring JAM Project, was held [^51].
- March 4 — The first Nicowari Game tournament was held using the Nicowari interruption feature.
- March 5 — The “Niconico Video (SP1) Presentation” was held at Akihabara UDX [^52].
- March 5 — **Niconico Video (SP1)** launched.
- March 6 — Sales of time-signal advertisements began on Niconico Video.
- March 17 — Total comments reached one billion [^53].
- March 28 — Trial distribution of “Niconico Sports,” delivering breaking sports news and similar video information, began jointly with Looped Picture and Data Stadium [^54].
- April 1 — The midnight time signal announced the end of Nicowari. As a one-day April Fools' joke, comments scrolled left to right rather than their usual right-to-left direction. The name became **Niconico Video (LOL) 2.0**. My Page and various minor elements also changed that day.
- April 1 — A blanket agreement covering the use of music administered by JASRAC was signed.
- April 1 — A service launched whereby entering “＠ピザ” (“@pizza”) opened a webpage for ordering pizza online.
- April 2 — The anime-only “Niconico Anime Channel” opened [^55].
- April 19 — Distribution began of *Penguin Musume Heart*, an original anime from Niconico Anime Channel.
- May 2 — Distribution began of *Candy boy*, an original anime from Niconico Anime Channel.
- May 9 — Various content partnerships with Yahoo! JAPAN were announced. Initially, Yahoo! Shopping products became available in Niconico Market, and Niconico Video appeared in searches on Yahoo! JAPAN and the Yahoo! Toolbar.
- May 12 — Video views reached five billion. [[Mirai Kensaku Brazil(Future Search Brazil)|Brazil]] launched the online encyclopedia “Niconico Pedia (Provisional)” [^56].
- May 14 — A DVD preview and stage greeting for the film *X-Cross: Makkyō Densetsu*, with a live appearance by Ami Suzuki, were broadcast live [^57].
- May 21 — The connection-speed measuring service Niconico Traffic Information launched.
- May 28 — The homepage, new videos, random search, and featured tags pages became viewable without an account. The Photozou partnership ended simultaneously.
- May 29 — Niconico News Maker launched, allowing users to make and submit their own news videos [^58].
- July 4 — The user event and press presentation “Niconico Daikaigi 2008: A Japanese Summer, a Niconico Summer” was held at JCB HALL (formerly Kōrakuen Hall) [^59] [^60].
- July 5 — Niconico Video (SP1) ended at noon. **Niconico Video (Summer)** launched at 18:00, together with **Niconico Community**, a feature for restricting video availability.
- July 9 — The compilation album *CD de Kiite Mite: Niconico Video Selection* was released [^61].
- July 18 — German and Spanish versions of Niconico Video launched, following the Taiwanese version [^62].
- July 23 — Animelo and dwango.jp mobile sites distributed “Niconico Video Ryūseimusume,” a version of “Niconico Video Ryūseigun” newly written for mobile content [^63].
- July 28 — The hip-hop, R&B, and reggae event “dwango.jp presents R-Festa2008” was streamed live [^64].
- July 29 — The first simultaneous opinion poll, the Nicowari Survey, took place.
- August 15 — **Niconi Commons**, an official system supplying and licensing materials to support creative work, launched.
- August 31 — The anime-song event “Animelo Summer Live 2008 -Challenge-” was streamed live [^65].
- September 1 — A blanket agreement covering the use of music administered by e-License was signed.
- September 3 — In collaboration with Microsoft, Niconico Messe, allowing viewing through Windows Live Messenger, and Niconico Alert, an information service using Windows Live Alerts, launched.
- September 30 — The new-feature presentation “Niconico Shōkaigi 2008” was held [^66].
- October 1 — Niconico Video (Summer) ended at 13:00, and random search was discontinued. **Niconico Video (Autumn)** launched at 16:00, adding User Nicowari.
- October 1 — An advance screening of episode 1 of the television anime *Akaneiro ni Somaru Saka* was held [^67].
- October 15 — **Niconico Points**, a point system usable for paid content; **Niconico Medal Game (working title)**, allowing games while watching videos; and ai sp@ce, a free communication service recreating worlds from *bishōjo* content in a 3D virtual space, launched [^68].
- October 21 — “A Word in the Upper Right” returned in the advertisement area of the playback screen.
- November 12 — Registered IDs exceeded ten million [^69] [^70].
- November 19 — The iPhone/iPod touch game “Niconico Iraira” was released [^71].
- December 4 — “Niconico Daikaigi 2008 Winter: The Day Before the Day After Tomorrow” was held at JCB HALL [^72].
- December 5 — Niconico Video (Autumn) ended at 06:00; **Niconico Video (Winter)** launched at 14:00.
- December 5 — Official videos and Niconico Community were integrated into **Niconico Channel & Community** [^73]. t
- December 12 — Niconico Video (Winter) ended at 11:00; **Niconico Video (ββ)** launched at 15:00, adding user and channel live broadcasts. Niconico Video also marked its second anniversary [^74].
- December 22 — **Niconico Square**, enabling real-time chat with large numbers of people, launched.

### 2009

- February 6 — A feature for viewing past rankings was added.
- February 12 — An online “Niconico Shrine” was established to pray for profitability [^75].
- February 14 — **Niconico Direct Sales** opened and began accepting preorders for a figma Billy Herrington figure [^76].
- March 10 — The user advertising service **Niconi Advertising** launched [^77].
- March 16 — Premium membership exceeded 300,000.
- April 1 — As an April Fools' project, **Niconico Video (βββ)** launched at midnight Japan time, with a high-speed player whose comments moved faster and whose color scheme turned red. Both reverted on April 2.
- April 1 — At 13:30, the homepage, viewing pages, and other layouts changed. Tag searches became possible directly from the homepage.
- April 10 — The first official adult channel, “DMM. Channel,” opened with content supplied by Digital Media Mart [^78].
- April 21 — The “Niconico Video × ELT Ichirō Itō Solo Project Vocal Audition” was held [^79].
- April 24 — The dungeon RPG “Niconico Tower” was released on the mobile game portal Keitai Game Hyakusen [^80].
- April 27 — Niconico Video Mobile released the Niconico Video iPhone app [^81] [^82].
- May 3 — Total views exceeded ten billion [^83].
- May 25 — Niconico Live began its first regularly scheduled weekday program, “Toriaezu Namachū (Provisional)” [^84].
- July 8 — Total comments reached two billion [^85].
- July 24 — The first trial of **Niconico Jikkyō** began.
- July 25 — Premium membership exceeded 400,000 [^86].
- July 27 — Niconico Channel & Community was relaunched as Niconico Channel, with Niconico Community separated onto its own pages.
- July 30 — The program “It's Summer! It's a Festival! It's Comments! Niconico Video's 12-hour Nonstop Live Broadcast” streamed.
- August 4 — Nicorepo Mail launched.
- August 7 — The third original anime from Niconico Anime Channel, *Ontama!*, was distributed.
- August 27 — Niconama Alert (Original) launched.
- September 19 — Premium membership exceeded 500,000 at around 10:00 [^87].
- October 5 — Time-shift viewing was implemented for official live broadcasts [^88].
- October 28 — The Niconico Video (9) presentation was held [^89].
- October 29 — Niconico Video (ββ) ended at 05:00; **Niconico Video (9)** launched at 09:30. Temporary Mylist, the post-playback menu, Nicorepo, Watchlist, and Niconama Alert (Player) launched.
- October 29 — The live program “The World's New Videos” began.
- November 14–February 19, 2010 — The “Niconico Daikaigi 2009–2010: Niconico Video (9) National Tour” was held.
- November 14 — **Niconico Seiga**, a bulletin-board site for posting still images, launched [^90] [^91].
- November 28 — Niconico Jikkyō formally launched [^92].
- December 12 — Premium membership exceeded 600,000 at around 14:00 Japan time [^93].
- December 23 — The live program “Niconama Cruise” began.

### 2010

- January 12 — **Niconico DVD**, a service for sharing comments on DVD footage, launched [^94].
- January 19 — Dwango acquired “nico.ms,” the URL-shortening service dedicated to Niconico Video [^95].
- January 30 — Games Arena began an open beta of its game service **Niconico Amusement Park**.
- February 6 — Niconico Telephone, allowing mobile users to call participants in live broadcasts, was announced.
- March 5 — Premium membership exceeded 700,000 at around 13:20 [^96].
- March 5 — Niconico Seiga (W) was upgraded and an illustration section, “Tried Drawing,” opened [^97].
- April 5 — The live radio program “Nicoradio” began.
- April 7 — Hakuhodo DY Media Partners and Dwango developed **Niconico Drama**, a video service allowing users to watch and comment on television-station content on Niconico Video before its broadcast.
- April 23 — The Government Revitalization Unit's second round of budget screening was streamed on Niconico Live with official approval from the Cabinet Office's Government Revitalization Unit.
- May 13 — Dwango's financial-results briefing streamed on Niconico Live.
- May 13 — It was announced that the Niconico Video business had achieved its first profit in the second quarter of fiscal 2010 [^98].
- May 14 — Premium membership exceeded 800,000 at around 21:30.
- May 17 — The Niconico Live iPhone app was released.
- June 16 — Niconico Live began live coverage of the House of Representatives.
- August 3 — Premium membership exceeded 900,000 at around 09:25.
- August 26 — The first ticketed event, “Niconico Daikaigi 2010 Summer: The Power of Smiles,” was held at C.C.Lemon Hall [^99].
- August 27 — “Tried Drawing Manga on Niconico” (now **Niconico Manga**) launched [^100].
- September 14 — Spike launched the game site **Nicogame** [^101].
- October 13 — Premium membership exceeded one million at around 19:50 [^102] [^103].
- October 28 — The Kadokawa Group and Niconico Video operator [[Dwango]] entered a comprehensive business alliance for the distribution of ebooks and other content.
- October 28 — “Niconico Daikaigi 2010 Autumn: Is That Rock?” was held at SHIBUYA-AX [^104].

![](https://upload.wikimedia.org/wikipedia/commons/6/67/Nico_Nico_Douga_logo.png)

Logo of Niconico Video (Harajuku).

- October 29 — Niconico Video (9) formally ended at 06:00 Japan time [^3]. **Niconico Video (Harajuku)** launched around 10:00, adding Stamps and other features [^105] [^106].
- October 29 — The news site **Niconico News** launched; the former Niconico News was renamed Niconico Info.
- November 3 — Niconico Live broadcast “Ichirō Ozawa's Online Press Conference: I'll Answer All Your Questions!” [^107].
- November 10 — Niconico Toolbar for Internet Explorer was released, allowing Niconico Video searches using Bing's video search [^108].
- November 11 — At the announcement of results for the year ended September 2010, the company announced Niconico Video's first full-year profit [^109].
- November 30 — “Niconico Dōka,” a confectionery collaboration with Lotte, was released nationwide.
- December 9 — The gaming platform **Niconico Apps** launched.
- December 12 — The first “Niconico Dance Master” *odottemita* dance festival was held at Laforet Museum Harajuku [^110].[^tn-temita]
- December 15–February 5, 2011 — The “Niconico Daikaigi 2010–2011 National Tour: Thank You, One Million People” was held.
- December 22 — The first Niconico Musical production, *A Christmas Carol*, was distributed as paid content.
- December 27 — The Niconico Headquarters promotional shop held a preliminary opening in Harajuku [^111].

### 2011

![](https://thumb.wikimedia.org/wikipedia/commons/thumb/9/91/%E3%83%8B%E3%82%B3%E3%83%8B%E3%82%B3%E6%9C%AC%E7%A4%BE_-_panoramio_%281%29.jpg/500px-%E3%83%8B%E3%82%B3%E3%83%8B%E3%82%B3%E6%9C%AC%E7%A4%BE_-_panoramio_%281%29.jpg)

Niconico Headquarters (2012).

- January — Premium membership exceeded 1.1 million [^112].
- January 8 — “Niconama Session♪” launched, using Yamaha's NETDUETTO β technology to allow musical sessions during live broadcasts.
- February 4 — “Nicoupon,” an interactive coupon service within Niconico Video supplied by Futurescope, launched.
- February 12–13 — “Namaket, the Festival of Live Broadcasters,” abbreviated “Namake,” was held [^113].
- February 14 — A Niconico Video television commercial featuring GACKT aired on terrestrial television [^114].
- March 27 — The volunteer-information site **Niconico Volunteer** opened.
- April 11 — The Niconico Headquarters promotional shop held its grand opening.
- April 17 — **Niconico β**, an independently operated English-language service separate from Niconico Video, launched.
- May — The opinion channel “Nico Rondan” opened.
- May 2 — A preview event was held for the first Niconico Movie, *Video Detective Great Special: The Niconico Serial Murders*.
- May 8 — Every bout of the May Grand Sumo Technical Examination Tournament was streamed live.
- May 22 — “Niconico Daikaigi 2011 in Taiwan” was held at Legacy Taipei.
- June 2 — **Niconico Remote Control**, allowing a mobile phone to control Niconico Video, launched [^115].
- June 19 — The *odottemita* festival Niconico Dance Master 2 was held at Differ Ariake.
- July 12 — The online-ticket information site Niconico Ticket Center opened.
- July 12 — The Vocaloid-focused page “Vocalo Music” opened [^116].
- July 18 — The live venue Nicofarre opened.
- August 2 — Niconico Video, Niconico Live, and Niconico Channel officially added Android support.
- August 11 — **Niconico Shunga** launched.
- September 12 — The smartphone voice-communication app Nicolson was released [^117].
- September 22 — Nico Auction was held on Mobaoku, with Niconico Video-related items offered for auction.
- September 27 — The first shopping program, “Niconico Shopping: Autumn ELECOM Festival,” aired [^118].
- October 1 — Niconico R18 Live (later Niconico Adult Live) launched. “DMM. Channel Presents Everyone's Toy” aired with DMM.com support.
- October 2 — The latest episode of *Fate/Zero* was simultaneously distributed worldwide through Niconico Live and Niconico Channel with subtitles in eight languages [^119].
- October 9 — Niconico Live offered advance distribution of the television anime *Future Diary*.
- October 14 — Nicogadget launched with support from Guguddo Marketing [^120].
- October 15 — The *odottemita* festival Niconico Dance Master 3 was held at Nicofarre.
- November 8 — Niconico Seiga (Ebooks) launched. Niconico Video and Kadokawa Shoten launched the web manga magazine *Kadokawa Niconico Ace* [^121].
- November 23 — Niconico Film Screenings began.
- November 26 — Niconico Headquarters installed “Nicopuri,” Niconico Video's original photo-sticker machine.
- December — The Niconico app for Hikari i-Frame 2 was released [^122].
- December 13 — The Creator Incentive Program launched. Commons Tree was relaunched as Content Tree.
- December 17 — The Niconico app for PlayStation Vita was released.

### 2012

- January 9 — Episodes 1 and 2 of the television anime *Aquarion Evol* aired simultaneously with TV Tokyo.
- January 14 — The first Shōgi Den'ō Match, “Lifetime Kisei Kunio Yonenaga versus Bonkras,” was held.
- January 24 — Niconico Live began live coverage of the House of Councillors.
- February 9 — The live-streaming software Niconico Live Encoder was released.
- March — Niconico Professional Baseball Channel opened.
- March 6 — The event-information site Nivent Calendar opened.
- March 13 — Manga submission was added to Niconico Seiga [^123].
- April 28–29 — The Niconico culture festival Niconico Chōkaigi and live event Niconico Chō Party were held at Makuhari Messe [^124] [^125].
- May 1 — **Niconico Video:Zero** and Niconico Live:Zero launched with early access for Premium members. The collective service name changed to **niconico** [^126].
- May 3 — The participatory shopping site Niconico Shopping (Provisional) launched.
- July 29 — The first Niconico Chōkaigi Town Meeting National Tour 2012 opened in Yazu, Tottori Prefecture.
- July 31 — **Niconico Video:Zero** and Niconico Live:Zero opened to ordinary members [^127].
- August 18 — The *odottemita* festival Dance Master 4 was held at Differ Ariake.
- August 21 — Niconico Channel launched “Blomaga,” a text-publishing service combining blogs and email newsletters [^128].
- September 25 — Panasonic launched the niconico app for Viera Connect.
- September 27 — Sony launched “Niconico Video: Pickup” for Appli Cast [^129].
- October 5 — In collaboration with Adways, the reward-advertising service “Get Niconico Points at a Bargain!” (now “Get Niconico Points!”) launched [^130].
- October 17 — **Niconico Video:Q** and Niconico Live:Q launched. The Taiwanese and English-language versions were integrated into niconico that day.
- October 24 — The Viera Connect niconico app added support for DIGA Blu-ray recorders [^131].
- October 26 — The Windows 8 niconico app was released.
- October 30 — Some features were updated. Playback history expanded from 20 to 30 entries, switched from cookies to account-based storage, and acquired a 90-day retention period.
- November 21 — The Android niconico app was released.
- November 18 — The main tournament of the participatory game event “Niconico Game Master with Shiren the Wanderer” was held at Nicofarre [^132].
- November 28 — The niconico app for Smart TV Box was released.
- November 29 — An online political party leaders' debate was held at Nicofarre.
- December 8 — The Niconico software for Wii U launched.
- December 20 — The recommendation feature Nicomend launched [^133].

### 2013

- January — Niconico Pro Wrestling Channel opened.
- February 4 — Uploads to Niconico Video exceeded 20 million [^134].
- February 18 — Hiroyuki Nishimura resigned as director/administrator of [[Niwango]] [^135].
- February 22 — Sanrio released “Hello Kitty × Niconico Video: Pack Your Own Tirol” chocolates.
- March — Niconico Video's advertising company Skyscraper became Smile Edge, a subsidiary of Kadokawa Group Holdings.
- April — The interactive puzzle-solving game “Niconico Headquarters Explosion” was held [^136].
- April 27–28 — Niconico Chōkaigi 2 and Niconico Chō Party II were held at Makuhari Messe [^137].
- April 28 — The first Niconico Homemade Game Festival awards ceremony was held.
- June 5 — Niconico Serial Novels launched [^138].
- June 22, 16:48 — Premium membership exceeded two million [^139].
- August 18 — The *odottemita* festival Niconico Dance Master FINAL was held at Differ Ariake.
- September 30 — The niconico iPhone app was released.

![](https://thumb.wikimedia.org/wikipedia/commons/thumb/f/f2/Nico_Nico_Douga_Ginza_Logo.svg/500px-Nico_Nico_Douga_Ginza_Logo.svg.png)

Logo of Niconico Video:GINZA.

- October 8 — **Niconico Video:GINZA** and Niconico Live:GINZA were released.
- October 10 — Commercial use of an Arcstar Universal One dedicated 100-gigabit Ethernet line began [^140].
- November 27 — Niconico Digital Copy (niconicoDRM) launched, allowing purchasers of eligible DVDs and Blu-rays supporting Walt Disney Japan's MovieNEX Club to watch the complete films on PC, iOS, and Android as a bonus [^141].
- December 2 — The “Niconico: Video and Live Broadcasts” app for Hikari BOX+ was released [^142].
- December 9 — Niconico Channel launched User Channels, a dedicated platform for ordinary users.

### 2014

- February 14 — The downloadable Niconico software for Nintendo 3DS was released.
- April 1 — MAGES. began operating animeloLIVE!, a paid service distributing live footage related to anime and voice actors [^143].
- April 10 — Sales of Niconico Video pre-roll video advertisements began.
- April 22 — Niconico Points went on sale through FamiPort multimedia terminals installed in FamilyMart stores.
- April 26–27 — Niconico Chōkaigi 3 and Niconico Chō Party III were held at Makuhari Messe [^144].
- April 30 — PlayStation 4's broadcast feature added Niconico Live support.
- May 1 — Niconico Seiga Print Workshop, an on-demand merchandise service, launched jointly with the Daiei Group.
- May 2 — The 3D submission and sharing service **Niconi Solid** launched.
- July 5 — The anime *Pretty Guardian Sailor Moon Crystal* was simultaneously distributed worldwide in 12 foreign languages.
- July 18 — Niconico Channel began serializing *3D Novel bell*, a reader-participation web novel jointly developed by dwango, KADOKAWA, and Group SNE.
- July 19–20 — The live program “Niconico 23-hour Television” streamed.
- August 5 — The Android app niconico Comment Viewer was released.
- August 8 — Niconico Smartphone SDK, a software development kit for smartphone apps, became available.
- August 12 — The niconico app for Bravia was released [^145].
- October 1 — KADOKAWA and Dwango merged their management to form KADOKAWA DWANGO [^146]. As a merger campaign, the Niconico Kadokawa Festival ran the “Visit a Bookshop for Lots of Good Things!” fair.
- October 6 — niconico and *Da Vinci* established the Next Manga Awards.

![](https://thumb.wikimedia.org/wikipedia/commons/thumb/a/a8/P%27PARCO_2018-10-25.jpg/500px-P%27PARCO_2018-10-25.jpg)

Niconico Headquarters (2018).

- October 25 — The Niconico Headquarters promotional shop relocated to Ikebukuro and reopened.
- November 17 — Videos incorporating Nintendo copyrighted works became eligible for the Creator Incentive Program.
- November 17 — Niconico Live launched virtual-reality live streaming using Oculus Rift.
- November 20 — The niconico app for Xbox One was released.
- November 21 — **Niconico Survey** launched.
- December 5–7 — “Niconico Kokkaigi: Japan Internet Culture Festival” was held in Singapore.
- December 12 — nicocas launched, allowing users to sign in with Twitter through the iPhone app and stream live video.
- December 12 — An Events feature was added to Niconico Channel [^147].

### 2015

- January 8 — The iOS version of NicoBox, an app specializing in audio playback, was released.
- January 31–February 1 — The first Tōkaigi festival for game fans and gaming competitions was held.
- February 8 — “Den'ō Match × TOYOTA: Real Car Shōgi” was streamed live.
- March — The visual-kei music program “Tokimeki☆Viju Night” was relaunched as “Nicoviju” [^148].
- March 19 — Dwango.jp Store opened, selling work by creators attracting attention on Niconico Video and elsewhere online.
- April 25–26 — Niconico Chōkaigi 2015 was held at Makuhari Messe [^149].
- May 27 — Niconico Game Magazine (now Game Magazine), a serialized service distributing new homemade games every week, launched.
- June 20 — The opening game of the first Eiō tournament was streamed live.
- June 25 — The niconico app for Android TV-equipped Bravia televisions was released [^150].
- July 5–August 30 — Nicobukuro Summer Festa 2015 was held at Niconico Headquarters.
- July 18–19 — “Niconico 23.5-hour Television” streamed live.
- July 28 — Premium membership exceeded 2.5 million [^151].
- July 30 — Niconico Documentary launched, distributing video works from around the world, primarily documentaries.
- August 5 — Registered IDs exceeded 50 million [^151].
- August 16 — Tommy Walker launched *Cerberus Blade*, a play-by-web game linked with niconico [^152].
- September 15 — The Android Niconico Manga app was released [^153].
- October 1 — Dwango absorbed Niwango in a merger.
- October 25 — Nicolun, a smartphone app for real-time chat with prominent Niconico users and celebrities, and **Niconare**, a service for creating and sharing narrated presentations, launched [^154].
- October 25 — Niconico Chō Party 2015 was held at Saitama Super Arena [^155].
- October 26 — Tagrepo launched.
- October 28 — The niconico app for Amazon Fire TV was released [^156].
- November 13 — The iOS Niconico Manga app was released [^157].
- November 28 — Dengeki Bunko and niconico launched the participatory multimedia project “Majority-Vote Drama.”
- December 10 — The niconico app for LG televisions was released [^158].
- December 18 — The niconicoVR app for Gear VR launched [^159].

### 2016

- January 20 — The smartphone app niconico event+ for in-person events was released [^160].
- February 8 — Denfaminicogamer, a gaming-information service from Dengeki, Famitsu, niconico, and 4Gamer, launched [^161].
- February 27 — The Niconico Odottemita Festival was held at Differ Ariake [^162].
- April 27 — Beta testing began for the Internet-café service Niconico @ Net Café [^163].
- April 29–30 — Niconico Chōkaigi 2016 was held at Makuhari Messe [^164].
- May — Niconico Horse Racing Channel opened [^165].
- May 25 — The niconico app for Sharp's AQUOS Cocoro Vision Player was released.
- June — Premium membership exceeded 2.56 million.
- July 7 — Version 3.00 of Niconico for Nintendo 3DS introduced live broadcasts and amiibo integration.
- August 2 — The immersive audio-streaming service LR NICOLIVE launched.
- August 3 — JOYSOUND × niconico's Karaoke Distribution Program began [^166].
- August 4 — The niconico app for Toshiba Regza was released [^167].
- August 26 — A partnership was formed with China Central Television, the People's Republic of China's state broadcaster [^168].
- October 4 — BookWalker launched the celebrity live-streaming platform Namatele [^169], integrated with niconico IDs and Niconico Points.
- October 27 — The HTML5 video viewing page beta was released exclusively to Premium members.
- November 3 — The iOS version of niconico ch was released [^170].
- November 3 — Niconico Chō Party 2016 was held at Saitama Super Arena [^171].
- November 24 — **RPG Atsumaru** launched.
- December 12 — Niconico Video marked its tenth anniversary.
- December 16 — Niconico and NHN PlayArt's joint game project *＃Compass: Combat Providence Analysis System* launched.
- December 17 — Niconico Live and SCRAP broadcast “Quiz! Answer with Comments,” a quiz show in which viewers' comments could secure victory [^172].

### 2017

- January — Real Derby Stallion, a project combining horse racing, games, and the Internet, began.
- February 27 — Sales began for an advertising package using Niconico Live × Twitter Promoted Live Video cross-media techniques [^173].
- March 3 — The Android version of niconico ch was released [^174].
- March 21 — Niconico Seiga (Ebooks) and COMIC ZIN began the Thin Books Project [^175].
- April 19 — The **Mastodon** service friends.nico launched [^176].
- April 29–30 — Niconico Chōkaigi 2017 was held at Makuhari Messe [^177].
- July 13 — The niconico software for Nintendo Switch was released [^178].
- July 13 — The HTML5 viewing page was formally released [^179].
- August 24 — The Creator Incentive Program added support for Niconico Live.
- September — A blanket licensing agreement was signed with Nintendo concerning Niconico Live broadcasts using Nintendo copyrighted works.
- October — The “Tsukasa Fushimi Channel” launched “Role-playing Q&A: Ayase Aragaki,” a conversational AI training project by NTT Media Intelligence Laboratories and Dwango [^180].
- November — The niconico app was installed on Cable Plus STB [^181].
- November 3 — Niconico Chō Party 2017 was held at Saitama Super Arena [^182].
- November 11 — The niconico app for Android TV-compatible Sharp AQUOS televisions was released [^183].
- November 28 — evaluni launched **Niconico Matome**, a video-focused curation platform [^184].
- November 28 — niconico TV for Apple TV was released [^185].
- November 28 — The new streaming and viewing system niconico (Ku) was presented. The features disappointed expectations, with no clear plan to address slow servers, image quality, or latency compared with other video services; both the venue and the video comments became highly hostile [^186] [^187].
- December 1 — The first unlimited-viewing anime channel, d Anime Store Niconico Branch, launched.
- December 12 — A “Discussion Meeting on Video and Live Services” was streamed. Shigetaka Kurita's appointment as the new operations representative and Kawakami's departure as head of niconico operations were announced. Subsequently, Kurita continued dialogue with users through live broadcasts and social media, and Niconico Live regularly streamed reports on niconico (Ku)'s progress and improvements to video and live services.
- December 28 — “Niconico Odottemita Festival Powered by Japan Airlines” was held at JAL's Haneda hangar [^188].

### 2018

![](https://thumb.wikimedia.org/wikipedia/commons/thumb/1/1f/Nico_Nico_Douga_Crescendo_logo.svg/500px-Nico_Nico_Douga_Crescendo_logo.svg.png)

Logo of Niconico Video (Ku).

- June 28 — **Niconico Video (Ku)** and Niconico Live (Ku) were released [^193].
- July 5 — **Niconico Q** launched [^194].
- July 24 — Stamps were revamped and Badges launched.
- August 1 — Niconico Live began Experimental Broadcasts supporting the new Gifts feature; the corresponding Android app nicocas (now Niconico Live) was released [^195].
- September 25 — Niconico Live became viewable without logging in.
- September 25 — The BOOK☆WALKER and Niconico Books smartphone apps merged as BN (BOOK☆WALKER・niconico) Reader. Sales through the Niconico Books web store ended, and BOOK☆WALKER introduced Niconico account login and Niconico Points payments [^196].
- October 1 — The final version of N Air, software for streaming on Niconico Live, was released [^197].
- October 3 — The VTuber smartphone streaming app Custom Cast launched, integrating with nicocas to simplify live streaming.
- October 11 — A Niconico News app was released.
- October — The ASMR aggregation site ASMR Village opened [^198].
- October 25 — Users became able to submit their own games for Experimental Broadcast Games in nicocas and Experimental Broadcasts [^199]. An Experimental Broadcast Game Contest was held accordingly.
- October 31 — Experimental Broadcasts became viewable without logging in.
- November 2 — Virtual Cast implemented a Niconico Video viewing item [^200].
- November 3 — Niconico Chō Party 2018 was held at Saitama Super Arena [^201].
- December 12 — Niconico marked its twelfth anniversary.
- December 14 — Niconico Pedia and [[pixipedia|Pixiv Encyclopedia]] held their joint “Internet Buzzwords 100” project at Nicofarre [^202].
- December 14 — Tommy Walker × niconico's play-by-web game *The Sixth Jaeger* launched [^203].
- December 18 — ARTILIFE, an artificial-life observation project allowing many users to observe simultaneously through nicocas live Experimental Broadcasts, was formally released [^204].

### 2019

- January 9 — Badges was publicly released (formal launch).
- February 6 — Selecting videos on one's Mylist page became easier [^205].
- February 7 — Niconare was relaunched as “niconare” [^206].
- February 23 — Virtual Cast released the alpha version of THE SEED ONLINE, a distribution platform usable across VR services and integrated apps [^207].
- March 7 — The PC version of Niconico Live added HTTPS support [^208].
- March 12 — New Stamps ceased to be issued. Previously obtained Stamps remained viewable on My Page.
- March 26 — The niconico app for Panasonic devices ended service.
- March 27 — A partnership with AbemaTV was signed. From April 1, chiefly original AbemaTV content, including dramas and variety shows, began distribution on Niconico Live and the Abema TV channel within Niconico Channel [^209].
- April 1 — Dengeki Bunko × niconico's *Engage Princess* launched on Niconico Apps [^210]. It ended service on September 30 that year [^211].
- April 11 — Gifts, Niconico New Market, and multicamera features were added to user broadcasts. Live Cruise, a revamped Niconama Cruise, launched. The live viewing page layout, poll activation conditions, and live player settings changed. Experimental Broadcasts was discontinued. niconare added an export feature [^212] [^213].
- April 22 — The design of the followed-communities page in Niconico Community changed [^214].
- April 27–28 — Niconico Chōkaigi 2019 was held at Makuhari Messe [^215]. Mastodon (friends.nico) ended service at the same time [^216].
- May 22 — Niconico Matome ended service [^217].
- May 30 — Niconico New Market and Gifts were added to official live broadcasts [^218].
- June 3 — Gifts and Niconico New Market were added to channel broadcasts [^219].
- June 13 — Uploaded videos could now be grouped into series [^220].
- June 26 — Video rankings were revamped and Custom Rankings launched [^221].
- August 29 — Nicoru returned exclusively for Premium members [^222].
- September 3 — A mandatory iOS app update raised the minimum supported OS to iOS 11.
- September 9 — A mandatory Android app update raised the minimum supported OS to Android 5.0 (Lollipop).
- September 12 — An experimental version of N Air became available [^223].
- September 25 — Keyword searches added synonym support [^224].
- October 3 — Nicoru (β) was released [^225].
- October 17 — Niconi Advertising added support for Niconico Pedia [^226].
- November 1 — A new satellite studio, Harestage, opened in Ikebukuro.
- November 6 — Time-shift viewing moved to the Niconico Live app [^227].
- November 28 — Wii U service ended.
- December 3 — The PC version of Niconico Live ended Internet Explorer 11 support [^228].
- December 11 — Live viewing moved to the Niconico Live app [^227].
- December 17 — Mandatory updates of the Android and iOS niconico apps accompanied the feature transfers [^227]. Service on PlayStation Vita and PlayStation Vita TV ended.
- December 24 — Previous and next videos in a series began appearing automatically [^229].

### 2020

- January 15 — A bar below video thumbnails began showing the last playback position [^230].
- January 16 — The smartphone app's login-screen design changed [^231].
- January 23 — Command blocking changed to hide comments decorated by the specified commands [^232].
- January 28 — Niconico New Market was renamed Broadcast Extras [^233].
- January 29 — Automatic video-volume adjustment was added [^234]. Niconico Market on video viewing pages was revamped [^235].
- February 5 — Nicorepo in the Android app added filtering and mute features [^236].
- February 12 — The midnight time signal ended [^237]. Premium members gained the option to hide the marquee area. Autoplay became available to ordinary members.
- February 13 — Niconi Advertising added a new smartphone advertising placement [^238].
- February 18 — The live program guide's specifications changed [^239].
- March 1 — Ordinary members became able to change thumbnails for videos uploaded in 2017 or earlier [^240].
- March 3 — Comments became viewable in N Air [^241]. Previous and next videos in a series began appearing on viewing screens in the iOS and Android apps [^242].
- March 12 — Smartphone-browser Niconico Video added access to other users' following and follower lists [^243].
- March 18 — Scheduled start times for user broadcasts could now be set in five-minute increments [^244].
- March 23 — The iOS app added access to playlists, following lists, and follower lists [^245]. Niconico Points became purchasable with American Express [^246].
- March 24 — The queued-video count, congestion indicator, aspect-ratio adjustment, and upload-time video translation were discontinued. The upload-page design was adjusted [^247], in preparation for future system changes.
- March 25 — A feature for continuous playback from the beginning of a series was added [^248].
- March 26 — Niconico Community's homepage began displaying communities likely to interest the viewer [^249].
- March 31 — A feature simplified setting custom rankings [^250]. The smartphone-browser header gained notifications [^251].
- April 1 — Playlists were added to the Android app [^252].
- April 7 — All Broadcast Extras became usable from user level 1 [^253].
- April 13 — The iOS app added tag editing, skipping, and adjustment of skip duration [^254].
- April 16 — The Android app's skip feature was revamped [^255].
- April 21 — More programs could appear on the live “Playing Live Games” page [^256].
- May 11 — Pushing Broadcast Extras off the screen no longer awarded user-level experience [^257].
- May 14 — Automatic video-volume adjustment was added to the smartphone-browser version [^258].
- May 20 — My Page was revamped in the Android app [^259].
- May 25 — The iOS version added dark mode [^260].
- May 26 — An alpha version of Emotions was released for Niconico Live, exclusively for Premium members [^261].
- May 28 — Previous and next series videos began appearing automatically in video descriptions in the smartphone-browser version [^262].
- June 4 — The uploaded-videos list was revamped in the smartphone-browser version [^263].
- June 15 — Live broadcasters gained or received adjustments to controls including enabling/disabling Emotions and their display duration [^264].
- June 16 — An Afterparty Venue feature was added to live broadcasts [^265].
- June 23 — The smartphone version of Niconico Pedia added article creation and editing [^266].
- June 24 — The beta version of Emotions was released, available with restrictions to ordinary members [^267].
- June 25 — Editing of profile self-introductions was revamped [^268].
- June 30 — Individual series could now be set public or private [^269].
- July 1 — Notifications of Blomaga comments added smartphone support [^270]. PC notifications and the header were revamped [^271]. Premium-exclusive videos began distribution [^272].
- July 6 — The maximum membership of Niconico communities doubled [^273].
- July 9 — Smartphone-browser users became able to post a scene from a video to Twitter [^274].
- July 14 — Users could configure a thank-you message for the Like feature added around the end of July [^275].
- July 27 — Niconico Video added Likes, Easy Comments, and Analytics; Niconico Live added Afterparty and Comment Filters [^276]. Previously separate live-broadcast “rooms” were merged. Visibility settings for published uploads and Mylists were abolished [^277]; these became public by default.
- July 30 — Registration using Yahoo! email addresses was temporarily suspended to address traffic considered an attack on Niconico Video [^278].
- August 3 — Followed-program notifications were repositioned in Niconico Live [^279].
- August 6 — Video-upload speed improved roughly threefold [^280]. A pre-broadcast guidance feature was added [^281].
- August 9 — **Niconico Video (Re)** and Niconico Live (Re) were released [^282].
- August 19 — Genre rankings were added to highest-ranking records [^283].
- August 25 — Likes began appearing in notifications [^284].
- September 1 — N Trend, a site aggregating niconico content, launched [^285]. Nintendo Network ID login ended [^286]. Nicoru was updated so that a comment liked by the uploader would be identified in the user's history [^287].
- September 2 — Notifications became viewable in the Android app [^288].
- September 16 — Niconi Solid discontinued its embedded player [^289].
- September 29 — Nicoru was added to the iOS and Android apps [^290].
- October 1 — Tag Editing Permission settings were added to control whether ordinary members could edit a video's tags [^291].
- October 6 — Following generated notifications [^292]. The iOS and Android apps added a data-saving viewing feature [^293]. “Other” was added to the gender field in Niconico Video Analytics [^294].
- October 13 — Niconico Video added the ability to post videos with comments to Twitter [^295].
- October 14 — Niconico Q became unavailable for requests through Broadcast Extras [^296].

![](https://thumb.wikimedia.org/wikipedia/commons/thumb/f/fa/Niconico%28Re%29.svg/500px-Niconico%28Re%29.svg.png)

Logo of Niconico (Re).

- October 27 — My Page fully migrated to the new version [^297]. The homepage was revamped [^298]. The collective service name changed from “niconico” to “**ニコニコ**” [^299]. New Emotions and Broadcaster Emotions were added [^300].
- October 29 — “Suddenly! Video Introductions” began appearing on Niconico Video's homepage [^301].
- November 5 — The Android app added Favorite Comments, pausing while entering comments, and saved commands [^302].
- November 19 — Niconico Live added adjustable comment opacity [^303].
- December 1 — Emotions began appearing in the comment list [^304].
- December 2 — Child works began appearing in Content Tree [^305].
- December 15 — The Flash video version ended because Adobe Flash Player was being discontinued [^306].
- December 16 — Niconico Jikkyō was relaunched within Niconico Live because of Flash Player's discontinuation [^307] [^308]. The Flash version of Niconico Live also ended [^309]. Advertising tickets began being awarded for a user's first video advertisement [^310].
- December 17 — A mandatory N Air update was issued [^311]. Live-broadcast page designs changed [^312].

### 2021

- January 19 — getplayerstatus was discontinued [^313].
- February 4 — RPG Atsumaru was renamed Game Atsumaru.
- February 25 — Live-broadcast Tagrepo ended [^314].
- March 1 — Past comment logs changed [^315].
- March 11 — Songle added Niconico-account login [^316].
- March 15 — Posting and displaying comments in My Memory and on deleted videos ended [^315].
- March 18 — Like counts became visible [^317]. Game Atsumaru released Multiplayer Games (alpha) [^318].
- March 30 — Viewers became able to follow preferred tags [^319]. The live app added an indication of whether viewers were present [^320].
- April 8 — A search feature for homemade games in Broadcast Extras was added [^321].
- April 12 — Niconico Community added blocking of users other than followers [^322].
- April 20 — Series thumbnails became configurable [^323].
- April 24 — Niconico Video set a record of 6,805 uploads in one day [^324] [^325].
- May 20 — My Page in the iOS video app was revamped [^326].
- May 26 — N Air gained comment text-to-speech [^327].
- June 1 — The video-editing page display changed [^328]. A continuous-playback button was added to uploaded-video lists [^329].
- June 9 — **Niconi Contribution** was released [^330].
- June 15 — Niconico Live's homepage was revamped [^331].
- June 17 — Game Atsumaru released multiplayer game submission [^332].
- June 29 — Like counts were added to video information [^333].
- July 12 — NHK BS1 was added to Niconico Jikkyō [^334].
- July 7 — Migration of the comment servers began [^335].
- August 4 — A page collecting Premium benefits was released [^336].
- August 11 — Live Premiere was released, streaming a video on Niconico Live when it was uploaded [^337].
- August 19 — Ordinary members' upload limits changed to expand according to user level [^338].
- September 2 — Comment-server migration was completed [^339].
- September 8 — Internet Explorer 11 support ended [^340].
- September 15 — Premium registration in the Android Niconico Video app added Google Play payment support [^341].
- October 7 — User Blomaga ended service [^342].
- October 13 — Gifts were added to Niconico Video [^343].
- October 27 — Popular past programs were added to the Android/iOS Niconama app's TOP screen [^344].
- November 1 — Niconico Channel's anime portal closed and was consolidated into N Anime [^345].
- November 30 — Niconico Video Mobile ended service [^346].
- December 1 — A Playback Speed button was added to the PC video player [^347].
- December 6 — Niconico Points went on sale at 7-Eleven, Lawson, and Ministop [^348].
- December 8 — Support for Android 4.4 and earlier ended [^349]. Icons began appearing over the player when keyboard shortcuts were used [^350]. Premium members gained a “last viewed position” indicator on video thumbnails [^351].
- December 12 — Niconico Video marked its fifteenth anniversary and opened a special website [^352].

### 2022

- January 7 — Test operation of Niconico Channel+, a new Niconico Channel feature, began on selected channels. Its first program, “Kaori Maeda's World Conquest Plan #1,” streamed live on January 13 [^353].
- February 28 — Restrictions on broadcasters requesting Broadcast Extras were removed [^354]. Broadcast Requests was added [^355].
- February 16 — Live My Page was integrated into the general My Page [^356].
- March 14 — Niconico Prize BOX ended [^357].
- April 21 — The following limit rose from 600 to 800 users [^358].
- June 23 — Membership Ranks were added to Niconico Channel [^359].
- July 20 — Comment deletion became available in the smartphone-browser version [^360].
- July 26 — Video replacement and batch uploads of up to five videos were added [^361].
- August 3 — Comment deletion became available on PC [^362].
- September 1 — The service menu at the right edge of the header was revamped [^363].
- September 13 — Ordinary members gained access, according to user level, to video-upload benefits previously reserved for Premium members [^364].
- September 26 — Comment deletion became available in the Android video app [^365]. The app also added hiding specified uploaders' videos in search results and rankings [^366].
- September 27 — The iOS video app added hiding specified uploaders' videos in search results and rankings [^367].
- September 28 — The PC version added hiding specified uploaders' videos in searches and rankings [^368], and a show/hide setting for content potentially containing sensitive material [^369].
- September 29 — The Nintendo Switch app added continuous playback using playlists without an account [^370].
- October 5 — The Creator Incentive Program was updated with improvements including smartphone support and a creator-income portal [^371].
- October 6 — Creator Support launched: anyone could support a creator for ¥330 monthly, and users at level 18 or above could receive support [^372]. User pages gained cover images [^373].
- October 16 — At the end of day 2 of Niconico Chō Party 2022, “eR” was announced as the first new Niconico Video version name in two years [^374].
- October 17 — Comment deletion became available in the iOS video app [^375].

![](https://thumb.wikimedia.org/wikipedia/commons/thumb/d/dc/Niconico%28eR%29.2.jpg/500px-Niconico%28eR%29.2.jpg)

Logo of Niconico (eR).

- October 26 — Gift Encyclopedia was released. Gifts sent on Niconico Live and Niconico Video could be registered and collected there [^376].
- December 12 — **Niconico Video (eR)** and Niconico Live (eR) were released.
- December 15 — Theater Mode (beta) was added to Niconico Live [^377].
- December 20 — A Live Broadcasts tab was added to user pages [^378].

### 2023

- January 16 — Niconico Community's community-creation page was revamped [^379].
- March 1 — Ordinary members gained access, according to user level, to Premium-only features such as extending live broadcasts [^380].
- March 16 — Comment deletion was added to the Nintendo Switch video app [^381].
- March 29 — The NFT marketplace NicoFT launched, selling limited quantities of the digital-asset creator-support item Niconico Partner Pass [^382].
- March 31 — Niconico for Nintendo 3DS ended service [^383].
- April 19 — A submission page for Niconama Games opened. New submissions, updates, and Niconico Points payments on Game Atsumaru were suspended [^384].
- May 23 — Niconico Live experimentally added picture-in-picture, allowing programs to be watched in a small independent window [^385].
- June 28 — Game Atsumaru ended service. In-game payments on Niconico Apps were suspended [^386].
- July 3 — The latest Android/iOS video app versions released that day added Chromecast support [^387].
- July 25 — Niconi Commons received a major update, including a Music Monetization Service [^388]. Live-broadcast history moved to Niconico Garage and Live My Page ended [^389].
- August 28 — Niconico Live released Name Tags [^390].
- September 27 — Niconico Apps ended service [^386].
- November 1 — A side menu was added to Niconico Video [^391].
- November 7 — Niconico Live search added broadcaster searches [^392].
- November 8 — Payments with some Mastercard cards were suspended [^393].
- November 28 — Broadcaster blocking on Niconico Live was revamped, making blocks of anonymous comments permanent as well [^394].
- November 30 — Niconico Market ended service [^395].

### 2024

- February 8 — Comment Gacha was added to Niconico Live's experimental features. It randomly selects one comment posted while active and displays the winner as a broadcaster comment [^396].
- March 1 — Monthly Niconico Premium membership rose from ¥550 to ¥790 [^397] [^398].
- March 15 — American Express payments were temporarily suspended [^399].
- March 25 — The video app's home screen was revamped [^400].
- May 10 — VISA payments began being temporarily suspended in stages [^401].
- May 24 — Diners Club payments were temporarily suspended [^402].
- May 28 — The “@pizza” feature ended [^403].
- June 8 — A major cyberattack temporarily halted Niconico Video and related sites. Other disruptions included the websites of operator Dwango and parent KADOKAWA becoming inaccessible [^404] [^405].

![](https://thumb.wikimedia.org/wikipedia/commons/thumb/8/8e/%E3%83%8B%E3%82%B3%E3%83%8B%E3%82%B3%E5%8B%95%E7%94%BBRe%E4%BB%AElogo.png/500px-%E3%83%8B%E3%82%B3%E3%83%8B%E3%82%B3%E5%8B%95%E7%94%BBRe%E4%BB%AElogo.png)

Logo of Niconico (Re:Provisional).

- June 14 — **Niconico Video (Re:Provisional)** launched [^404] [^406]. Intended as a temporary service until recovery, it included only essential features. Initially it displayed popular videos uploaded in 2007, replacing the selection every few days thereafter.
- July 12 — The version-specific chat service Niconico Square (Re:Provisional) launched [^407].

Logo of “Niconico Is Back.”

- August 5 — **Niconico Is Back** launched [^408]. Niconico Video and related services resumed [^409]. Temporary sites released as (Re:Provisional), including Niconico Video (Re:Provisional), closed [^410]. Niconico Community effectively ended because data and systems required for recovery had been lost [^409] [^411]. Nicorepo also ended because resuming it was considered difficult.
- August 8 — New from Followed Accounts, Nicorepo's successor, launched ahead of its announced late-August schedule [^412].

![](https://thumb.wikimedia.org/wikipedia/commons/thumb/3/37/Niconico%28Return%29.svg/500px-Niconico%28Return%29.svg.png)

Logo of Niconico (Re:turn).

- September 17 — **Niconico Video (Re:turn)** was released.
- October 16 — Niconico Live released Thumbnails [^413].
- October 29 — The maximum upload size rose from 3 GB to 6 GB. The upload tag-setting screen also began showing how many videos used the same tag and identifying similar tags [^414].
- October 30 — Access became unavailable from some foreign countries and regions.
- November 18 — Niconico Live released passphrase-protected limited-distribution streaming [^415].
- November 21 — The final version of Nicoru was released [^416]. Ordinary members gained restricted access, and users began receiving notifications when their comments were liked [^417].
- November 26 — Recovery of Badges was abandoned and the service ended [^418].

### 2025

- January 16 — Niconico announced that Niconico Shunga would close on January 29 and all submitted illustrations would be deleted [^419]. Existing contributors were urged to save their data [^420]. Niconico cited the social environment and international situation [^419], as well as foreign laws [^420], as background factors.
- January 19 — A total of 53,998 videos were deleted, reducing the overall view count by 902,291,730 [^421]. The explanation was that this implemented the terms and guidelines revised on October 30 the previous year [^422].
- January 29 — Niconico Shunga closed as announced, and all illustrations were deleted [^420].
- February 21 — VISA payments resumed after roughly nine months [^423].
- May 28 — **Niconico (to i)** was released [^424].
- October 8 — The chat service Niconico Square (β) was released [^425].
- October 10 — Niconico Premium membership serial codes went on sale on Amazon [^426].
- October 21 — New support plans were added to Creator Support [^427].

### 2026

- April 15 — Niconico Shorts, a vertical short-video feature, launched in the iOS/Android Niconico Video app [^428].
- June 25 — A dedicated Shorts viewing page was released for PC Niconico Video [^429].
- July 1 — Unlimited reading of 100 manga and light-novel volumes within BOOK☆WALKER became a Niconico Premium benefit [^430].
- August 1 — Monthly Niconico Premium membership rose from ¥790 to ¥990 [^431].

- Subtitle display [^417].

## Revenue

Most Niconico revenue comes from Premium membership, for which enrollment began in June 2007 and billing in July. In the year ended March 2018, Premium membership accounted for 78% of portal-business revenue [^432]. Other sources include banner and over-video advertisements, introduced in July 2007; Niconico Points revenue, introduced October 15, 2008; and monthly paid subscriptions to Niconico Channel [^433] [^434].

KADOKAWA, which oversees Niconico's operating company, aims to strengthen revenue sources independent of Premium membership numbers.

In its launch year, the year ended September 2007 (October 2006–September 2007), the portal business recorded revenue of ¥196 million and an operating loss of ¥671 million [^435].

Efforts to recruit paying Premium members and attract advertisements produced a monthly profit in December 2009. At the May 13, 2010 financial-results presentation, director Takeshi Natsuno announced the first quarterly profit, for January–March 2010 [^436]. Natsuno said: “I've never heard of a user-uploaded video service becoming profitable anywhere in the world. I think this may be close to a world first” [^437]. In the first profitable full year, ended September 2010 (October 2009–September 2010), the portal business recorded revenue of ¥6.335 billion and operating profit of ¥23 million [^438].

For the year ended March 2018, the web-services business, which includes the portal business, returned to a loss because of declining paid membership and delays in introducing new services.

An announcement on February 13, 2019 disclosed that monthly paid Niconico Channel subscribers numbered 911,759 as of December 2018, roughly 6.5 times the number five years earlier. Cumulative revenue since launch had grown roughly thirtyfold over five years to ¥12.511 billion, reflecting revenue sources beyond Premium membership [^439].

For the year ended March 2020, the web-services business, including the portal business, returned to profitability following improvements in its revenue structure [^440].

## User numbers

Accounts are issued for each registered email address, so one person can hold multiple ordinary accounts by using different addresses. Paying the fee for each account also permits multiple Premium accounts; there is no restriction on holding multiple accounts.

By November 2007, less than a year after launch, the service reportedly already accounted for roughly one-twelfth of Japan's total Internet traffic [^441], illustrating its rapid growth.

In JustSystems' April 2017 monthly tracking survey, LINE was the social-networking service most commonly used by teenagers, at 88%, followed by YouTube at 81%. Niconico Video ranked eighth at 20.0% [^442].

On May 30, 2025, the Ministry of Internal Affairs and Communications designated Dwango (service name: Niconico) as a large-scale specified telecommunications service provider, a category for services averaging at least ten million monthly users [^443] [^444].
